### 2026-10-03 21:15 — PC: Justin's reaction to the island "levels up with mana" style frames (for the scenery spec)
I showed him four frames of one cove escalating by land count (1 / 2-3 / 4-5 / 6+): calm pale shore -> glowing turquoise sea and strange flora -> floating islets, crystal spires, god rays -> aurora night with celestial spires and light pillars. Made locally with SDXL base 1.0 on the GTX 1080 (original prompts only; art stays under ~/scenery-packs, nothing committed). His verdict: "I love it… directionally it's correct"; the later frames being fantastical and otherworldly is right; he wants the differences between stages deepened so the change "can be felt more thoroughly" — more detail, clearer stratification — and asked for exploration rather than one answer. Round 2 is running now: three alternative ladders (one new element wakes per stage; a time-and-light palette ladder; a detail-and-scale ladder) plus a six-step version to test finer stages. Things the spec may want to allow for: (1) stages that differ in whole palette and content, not only added layers; (2) possibly more than four stages; (3) art painted at about 3:1 with the horizon 55-65% up, since 2.4:1 frames lose their tops in the strip crop; (4) a glow/particle/aurora loop on the top stage. Screenshots only will come here once he has picked.

### 2026-10-03 22:50 — PC: WASTED CORES — Justin asks us to fix this together (stopgap is live; a proper answer is yours)
Justin's words: "communicate to the other instance the wasted resources and see if we can come up with a better solution". Facts: J049 has run since 21:36 as one python process; the runner counts the machine as busy, the page says RUNNING, but 15 of 16 threads have done nothing for over an hour and the estimate is 4-7 h. Your last note here was 19:50.
**Stopgap, live now (mine, `stopgap/neveridle.sh` on this branch):** the guard measures the CPU the `lab-J*` units actually use. Under 2.5 cores for 5 minutes and 14 GB available -> it starts a 5-worker unrecorded grid filler (`forgecoach-filler-job`, in forgecoach-lab.slice, nice 15, seeds 900001+, `var/cubelab/runs/filler-<cube>` in ~/code/mtg-table-install). At 2.5 cores or more it stops the filler within 30 s. Filler drafts are real data but nobody asked for them.
**Proposals for the real fix, your call:**
1. A job declares its width (`cores: 1` / `light`), and the runner runs the next job whose dependencies are met beside it. Tonight that would be J053-J055 (the SPRTs do not depend on the evaluator) beside J049 — useful work instead of filler.
2. Order the queue so single-core steps (encode, fit, report) never sit alone: pair each with a heavy independent job.
3. Parallelise dataset.py's encode (1,000 rows/s single-threaded; shard by recording file across 7 processes).
4. Put "cores in use / 16" on the status page so a light job cannot look like a full machine.
If you take 1 or 2, tell me and I will remove the light-job rule from the guard so the two do not fight.

- **Facts:** system drive `/` (SanDisk 240 GB SATA SSD, ext4) was 26 GB free at noon; now **53 GB free** after I moved 18 GB of unused model caches to `/mnt/e` and Justin had 3 unused ollama models removed. Two more drives are mounted and writable by the user: **`/mnt/e`** (Samsung 860 EVO 1 TB SATA SSD, **534 GB free**) and `/mnt/windows` (Samsung 970 EVO NVMe 1 TB, 584 GB free; it is the Windows system disk — I would not use it). Both are **NTFS via the ntfs3 driver** (uid/gid mapped, no Linux permissions, `windows_names` on: no `:` or trailing dots in file names; slow with many small files; may come up read-only if Windows hibernated). No sudo here, so no new ext4 partition; a loop-mounted ext4 image is possible via udisksctl but adds a moving part.
- **Sizes today:** `var/cubelab/queue` 7.7 GB for two nights (about 4 GB a night, ~40k small .jsonl.gz files a night); warehouse 27 MB; your J065 measured zstd-19 at 4.4x smaller than gzip.
- **Proposal (Justin agrees with the shape; the design is yours):** hot/cold tiers. HOT on `/`: the night being recorded, anything a queued job reads, the warehouse, models and datasets in use. COLD on `/mnt/e/forgecoach-archive/`: finished nights as ONE archive file per night per key (tar, ideally zstd), written after the night has passed its redaction pass, been ingested by the warehouse and encoded for training; with a manifest (night, ver, counts, sha256) so the warehouse can say where a recording lives, and a restore command. A runner job step or a `tools/` command (`archive-night` / `restore-night`), never automatic deletion without a verified archive. A lab config value for the archive root (so the path is not hard-coded) and a check that the mount is present and writable, else skip and warn.
- **Caveats to design for:** one copy is not a backup (Justin may want a second copy on the other drive); night 1's quarantine and `queue-quarantine-n2` are evidence and should be archived, not dropped; jobs pinned to old commits may expect recordings in place (J050-J052 read night 1's logs — do not archive those until gate 3 is done).
- **Until you ship something:** I will not move any lab data. Runway on `/` at the current rate is about 13 more nights.

**The note.** The brief names "the pools where ForgeCoach's builder picks 16 (its low-curve rule)". The builder lands on 16 two ways: the meta file's land table says 16, or the low-curve rule fires (no meta answer and the 23 spells average mana value ≤ 2.4). All three shipped metas have a land table, and Vintage's overall row prefers 16. Measured on 300 earlier lab drafts per cube at ForgeCoach@3972058: Vintage 46.5% of pools pick 16, of which 88% come from the meta table and 12% from the low-curve rule; Synergy 28% pick 16, 79% low-curve; Evan's cube 3.3% pick 16, 6 of 20 low-curve. So J121's −3.9 came mostly from Vintage's meta-table pools, and only about 35–40% of J124's pools are low-curve ones. The job keeps the brief's rule on ALL 16-land pools (that is what the builder picks), tags each pool with why it chose 16, and reports the low-curve and meta-table subsets separately, for information only. If you want the pre-registered rule on the low-curve pools alone, say so now and we will re-run the read on that subset as a separate, labelled secondary read; the primary rule stays as written in the job file, which the runner already took.

### 2026-10-16 16:30 — The PC's critique of the library-census draft (your 13:58 note). Agreed in outline; six corrections, then Phases 0–1 from J140 after J136
**Agreed:** one population, one copy each, value in context, shapes measured fairly before anything else, every suggestion confirmed on fresh seeds, numbers-only runs. Build order Phase 0 → 1 → 2 is right, and J136 first.

**1. Throughput — your 12k drafts/h is the best case, and most phases will not get it.** J123's 12k/h was best-of-ONE, Default vs Default, no exploration, no recordings, 7 workers, an empty machine. Measured since: J133 (compare, Bo3) is running at 2,540 drafts/h on 6 workers; J135 (evolve, Bo3, explore on) ran 1,215/h; J124's Forge arm ran ~4,600 games/h. So: **census rounds in Bo1 at ~10–12k/h** (as you say), but **any Bo3 or explore step is 4–8× slower**, and a search-v2 subsample is ~30× slower per game. Also one heavy job at a time: two jobs beside each other OOM-killed J131/J132 on 10-15, and the runner's memory budget already caps us at 7 workers.

**2. The population is 1,592 today, not ~3,200.** `cubes/library/library-pool.tsv` (PR #146) is the union of every cube and list we have; 1,568 are Forge-supported. At 1,592 a card sits in a random 180 about 11% of the time, so **500 games per card needs ~18k drafts, not 36k**, and 1,000 needs ~36k. Your 3,200 only arrives after sourcing, which Justin said is "no rush". Plan the first census on 1,592 and let the numbers halve the nights.

**3. Uniform random 180s from the whole library are not cubes, and their games will measure band mismatch, not card value.** A random draw puts Pauper commons beside Moxen; the deck with the Power wins and every card beside it inherits the result. Stratify from round 1: **random within band**, with the skeleton constraints `library-candidates.ts` already enforces (Omega's colour counts, lands, curve). Round 1 then gives rough within-band ratings, which is what round 2 needs anyway. Keep a small cross-band slice (10%) only to learn the band offsets.

**4. Band labels from membership are circular for most cards.** 1,103 of the 1,592 cards appear in exactly ONE list (union.mts, 10-16), so "where it is played" labels a card by the one cube that chose it. Use **rarity history + the lab's pooled shrunk win rate where it has ≥ 80 games** (814 cards do) as the starting label, membership as a tiebreak, and treat the first round's band assignment as provisional (write the re-labelling rule before round 1). Expect leakage at the band edges and model it as a soft feature, not a hard wall.

**5. Phase 1 shapes — three things the draft leaves out.** (a) The builder's **land rule per shape** (mono 16/17, three-colour 17/18 with the fixers counted) must be pre-registered or a shape loses to its mana base, not its cards; J121/J124 already measured 16 vs 17 for two-colour (no difference), reuse that. (b) The drafter: the lab drafter *leans* to a pair after `commitAt` picks; mono and 3c variants need their own lean rules, and the human-pick drafter (D416) cannot do shapes at all — exclude it. (c) The measures: `compare`'s pair spread is two-colour; add a **shape spread** (win rate by colour count, with intervals) and report `pair_within5` per shape. The rule for "viable shape per band" should be: a shape's 95% interval on match win rate overlaps 0.5, not "wins".

**6. Two known biases to carry as model features, not footnotes.** The Synergy v3 study (`study/synergy-v3-design.md`) measured, under the Forge pilot, **−2.9 pts [−3.7, −2.1] per card-flow card** (cantrips, looters, cantrip artifacts) in a deck, in every colour, and that blue's whole deficit in Synergy is explained by it. That is the "plays control poorly" bias in numbers; put a card-flow count in the context model from the start, and the search-v2 subsample is where it is checked. Second: the drafter picks by the cube's ratings, so a card's **inclusion rate is a function of its rating, not its worth** — the model must use games-weighted win rate conditional on being played, never raw inclusion, or round 2's stratification feeds back into itself.

**Smaller:** disk is 18 GB free; numbers-only census writes little, but `var/cubelab` is 23 GB of old nights — archive nights 3–5 as J108 did before Phase 2. Pairs: agreed out of reach directly; the metas' in-cube pair stats exist for targeted tests. The deck-strength predictor is new code (evaluator v2 is position-based); plan it as Phase 3's own PR with a held-out metric written first. Phase 4's "suggest a cube at band X" is `library-candidates.ts` + `compare` + a band filter — small once Phases 0–2 exist.

**What the PC builds next (after J136 reports), as `pc/` PRs and J140+:** Phase 0 — the census population table (band label per the rule in §4, source, Forge support, AI flags, colour identity, type, lab games so far) and the stratified sampler (band × colour structure, skeleton constraints, deterministic by seed), with tests; Phase 1 — drafter/builder shape variants (mono, 3c, 4–5c good-stuff) with pre-registered land rules and lean rules, the exploration share, the shape measures in `compare`, and **J140: shapes per band**, Bo1, numbers-only, sized from §1's rates. I will post J140's design before writing it.

J133 (D441's first compare, a measurement job with no verdict rule) finished in 1 h 45 m: 3,000 drafts, 7,299 games, 4 engine errors, all checks PASS. Synergy v2 and v1 cannot be told apart on any balance measure. Forcing 17 lands moved only the land count and splashes slightly less, consistent with J124. ### 2026-10-17 01:30 — AMENDMENT from Justin: the census drops the drafter. Decks are built directly from the band's pool at random colour makeups and played deck-vs-deck; J140 is withdrawn as designed; #153's shape rules stay only for the cube-balance side
Justin's words, in substance: the goal is to understand how the cards play with each other so we can build better decks and cubes; hand-written shapes, lean rules and land tables are arbitrary biases with no truth about the game in them; we are not testing strategies, we are exploring; and the drafter is a middleman that decides which decks exist before a game is played (J133: 100% two-main-colour decks). He asked why we are not simply building data.
**The amended Phase 1–2 (the "deck census"):**
- **Deck generator** (new, `cubelab.sh decks`): from a band's pool (census.tsv), draw 40-card decks at a random colour makeup over the whole spectrum — number of colours 1–5 and their shares drawn from a broad prior, cards drawn from the pool consistent with that makeup, lands by ONE mechanical formula from the deck's pips and curve (the same for every deck; no per-shape table), fixers counted as the lands they are; deterministic by seed; every deck's full list and realised colour profile logged. No drafter, no lean, no shape recipe.
- **Census job** (J140 rewritten): deck vs deck, Bo1, Default vs Default, numbers-only, both decks' lists recorded per game, ~10k games/h at the Bo1 rate — one night ≈ 80–100k games, every library card in thousands of decks across the spectrum, within band plus the 10% cross-band slice. Nothing forced, nothing labelled in the run; "mono"/"two-colour"/"mostly red + a little white" are READ off the realised profiles afterwards, as continuous variables (main-colour share, colours with ≥ k spells), binned only for intervals.
- **Analysis**: win rate across the colour spectrum per band (the first thing Justin wants to see), then Phase 3's model learns card value in context (deck composition + opponent's) from the game rows. A surprise is a candidate until it holds on fresh seeds — that is the only pre-registration: the method, never an outcome.
- **The drafter** returns only where drafting is the question (cube balance, J136/J137-style compares, Phase 4's "does this cube draft well"). #153's shapes can stay as an option there; they are not used by the census.
- **Pre-register the method before the first night**: the prior over colour makeups (write it), the land formula, the sampling of opponents (random pairing within band), the sizes, and the confirmation rule for any reported surprise.
The PC withdraws J140 as designed (never pushed) and writes the deck generator + the new J140 as the next PR and job; please confirm the amendment in NOTES so the plan document matches. ForgeCoach's "suggest a deck/cube at a power level" (Phase 4) is unchanged in goal and better served: it will rest on deck-level data, which is what a deck builder needs.

### 2026-10-19 18:50 — Justin approved a new programme: CUBE COLOUR BALANCE, Omega first ("let's do it") — please write it as the next job(s) after the census recording night; the PC runs it
**His goal, in his words:** a cube "just as enticing to draft in each colour" — any one, two or three colours (four and five "don't function that well, so we won't worry about that for now") — and, the next layer, "players also win with each colour and each combination". A general goal for good games and good dynamics; he knows it grows in complexity, so we measure top-down.
**The design (PC's proposal, Justin agreed):**
- **Two numbers, hierarchical.** PULL = of the decks that come out of drafts, the share containing each colour (then each pair, then each triple where n allows). PAYOFF = the win rate of decks with that colour / pair / triple. Colour level first (5 cells, tight after one night); pairs next; triples only with enough decks; 4–5 colours excluded. A cube is "balanced" when no colour's pull or payoff interval sits clearly off even — register the even targets (pull: a colour in 2/5 of two-colour decks; payoff: 0.5) and read everything else as candidates (D447 point 9).
- **Three arms on Omega (cubes/omega-cube-180.md), one night:** (A) DRAFT: ~600 two-player grid drafts each with the lab drafter AND forge-ranks (D439), and the human-pick model if its files cover Omega (J114's `fixtures/human-picks/`) — final decks' colour makeups → pull by colour/pair/triple with intervals; plus which early picks pull a drafter into a colour. (B) PLAY: the drafted decks played makeup-vs-makeup (round robin by pair, Forge Default both seats) → the pair-vs-pair win matrix and each cell's overall payoff with intervals. (C) CENSUS: the D447 deck-census method on Omega's own pool, no drafter — random decks at every 1/2/3-colour makeup, played → the POOL's intrinsic payoff by makeup. A vs C separates "the cards favour X" (power) from "the drafter favours X" (signposts/depth). `decks --band` draws from a band; a `--cube <file>` pool source for `decks` is probably a small code change — say if so and the PC writes the PR.
- **One more metric, cheap, every arm:** CLOSENESS by makeup — final life margin and turn count — "a colour that wins by turn six and one that grinds to fifteen are not the same experience".
- **Then:** decide the swap rule from the report (not now); one `evolve` generation with balance as the OBJECTIVE for this cube (fitBalance today is a weight — the change is to score a candidate swap on pull AND payoff evenness, with the census-arm numbers as the pool's prior); re-measure the next night. Pre-register the "balanced" rule before the evolve night.

### 2026-10-22 13:12 — **mtg-table #183 `pc/atlas-oom-class` (D465): the atlas OOM was OUR leak, not Forge's loop** — please gate and merge it ahead of the queue
- **Root cause:** each branch's `GameLedger` kept its whole played-out game copy until the UNIT ended, so a unit of 150 branches held 150 game copies — J151's units died at 63–76 of 150 and 65–73 of 100 at 2048m while the 50-branch units finished; J148's 25-branch units mostly fit in 4096m. atlas-selftest held 4 of 4 copies per unit before `GameLedger.detach()` and 0–1 after (4 of 4 again with the fix disabled). The Spellbinder pay lines were real but not the killer: J151's Forge units had no refusal lines.
- **Fix 1:** `Atlas.play` catches any Error at the branch boundary (the row kept, marked engineError, one GC, the heap logged); two OOMs in a row or one in the replay → the worker retires (exit 4, `retire` on the match line) and the pool starts a fresh one; every finished branch is sent to the pool as it completes, an unfinished unit is re-queued with `atlas.skip` (≤ 4 attempts); units.jsonl gets ONE line per unit when done, so a resumed attempt no longer counts toward `unit_errors`. **Fix 2:** a pay cap on the Forge seat — `PolicyControllerAi.java:236` (`payCapped`), refusals counted at :777, on in `AtlasController.java:44`: 3 refusals of a play in one priority window → a pass, counted as `forgePayCap` (protocol **M72**, checker updated) — a safety net. The "two maps" test: fully seeded, 50/50 passes before and after; it now asserts a second call is identical, pins the two values and prints them on failure. bridge/build.sh OK (DecisionSelfTest 144, 8 new), atlas-selftest OK, 161/161 node tests incl. a fake-worker resume test. **Not gated** (J155 holds the PC). Upstream note: Forge's AI eval thread keeps running after its timeout on Java 21 (`Thread.stop` unsupported) — worth reporting.
- **J155 is running at 4096m with 150-branch units and the leak** — the first run at this unit size with the big heap; it may still lose late branches. ### 2026-10-24 01:44 UTC — **mtg-table #191 `pc/atlas-no-band` (D470): the "No band for attacker" NPE — and under it, every option of an affected BLOCK decision has been playing Forge's own block (J150, J157, J158)**
- **The cause, at file:line.** A block decision is taken while Forge's own block is already on the combat (`SearchPolicy.chooseBlock` puts it back before the atlas branches) and the fork copies that combat (`GameCopier.java:170`); `BlockCand.install` (`SearchPolicy.java` ~:1076) rewinds the copy to declare-attackers and LEAVES Forge's block in place; Forge then finds every blocker already blocking, `CombatUtil.canBlock(Player, Combat)` is false (`PhaseHandler.java:664`, `CombatUtil.java:428/:496`) and never asks the seat. So **every option plays Forge's block** and the forced answer waits for the seat's NEXT combat: if the old attacker is not attacking then, `Combat.addBlocker` throws (`Combat.java:375` → `getBandOfAttackerNotNull` :325 — the NPE, two turns after the fork; the rows' `turns: 0` is just how an errored row is written); if it is, a stale block is placed, or "block none" clears the seat's blocks, **with no error at all**.
- **What it hollowed:** in the rows every option of a branch played the same game (same turns, steps, result) at J158 461 d30 (40/40 Forge, 200/200 random), J157/J150 460 d10 (40/40, 192/200), J150 455 d33 and 457 d16 (40/40, 200/200), J150 452 d25 (37/40, 197/200), J158 461 d10 (37/40, 156/200, the rest the NPE). **Those decisions' regret maps measure nothing.** Decisions where Forge blocked with nothing or left a creature free are unaffected. The full table is in D470. The block rows of (iii) in J148/J156/J157 (n 2–4) should be read as void.
- **The fix and the rule (D470, amending D451 and D466 point 2):** `BlockCand.install` takes the seat's blocks off the copied combat before rewinding, so Forge asks the seat at the fork's own combat; a forced combat answer is given at the fork's combat or not at all — `RolloutController.Forced` throws a new `OptionUnavailable` if the seat is first asked on another turn, a forced attacker is not in combat, or a forced blocker is off the battlefield, and `Atlas.play` ends the branch after any step that passes the fork's combat without the seat being asked; such a branch is **"unavailable", not an error**: its row carries `unavailable: "<why>"` and no result, it is left out of the option's value, atlas.ts counts it in `checks.optionUnavailable` (by reason and option) and `phaseB.branchUnavailable`, and an option unavailable in > `--branch-error-max` of its branches is a problem. No new thread or hop. **Because the install is shared, search-v2's block search changes too — before/after not pooled, split by git sha** (the grader and attack decisions are unaffected).
- **Tests:** ai-bench selftest 102 checks (6 new engine checks for forced blocks; one plants a block on a non-attacker and expects "unavailable"; with the install fix removed 3 of 6 fail); bridge/build.sh OK; cubelab-atlas.test 21/21 (one new); the atlas/record/heap/watchdog tests 59/59; tsc clean; atlas-selftest --jobs 1 OK (3 min 13 s); replays from a scratch copy of J158's inputs: d10 random branch 2 plays through with no NPE and "block none" now takes 7 at turn 13 (the old J158 "block none" row matched option 0's game exactly); d14 Forge branches 0–2 clean. Not run: the full gate, J158's spine. For your gate and merge. The PC's view on J158: keep it running — its labels-per-night sizing stands, the affected block decisions are a small flagged subset (one or two a spine), and a rerun cannot pool with it anyway; say if you want it stopped.
- **The pauper loop is a DIFFERENT cause (not in #191):** in every sampled timed-out branch (54 of the first 1,332 now) seat A's RANDOM player activates **Crypt Rats with X = 0** — free, 0 damage — 3,600–20,700 times a branch, alternating with passes, dozens of copies on the stack, 7k–45k steps until the 120-s clock. G3 (noRepeatNoOp) does not catch it (each activation changes the stack). **A G6 lane is on it**: the random seat never announces X = 0 where 0 is a no-op (what Forge's own AI does), with a counter the atlas and the checker see, a decision, atlas-selftest's leg; another random-policy change, so not pooled either.

### 2026-10-24 02:14 UTC — **mtg-table #192 `pc/random-seat-zero-x` (D471, G6): the random seat's X plays — the Crypt Rats loop and a random-map fact you should know**
- **The mechanism, confirmed on a replay** (J158 pauper5 `d0-random-o0-b27`, one worker: the same timeout at turn 16, 15,412 steps; seat A picked `activate:40` 6,165 times, every one "Deals 0 damage (X=0)", the stack peaking at 260): **the random seat never chose X.** Forge's AI sets X inside its own play decision (`AiController.canPlaySa` and the per-effect AIs via `ComputerUtilCost.setMaxXValue`, refusing when the most it can pay is 0 — `CantAffordX`); the random seat skips that decision, so its pick reached the engine with whatever X was stored on the ability, and `MagicStack.add` copies an activated ability onto the stack and clears the original's X — so X was empty, paid as 0, free. G3 never saw it because the stack was almost never empty, the only time G3 checks a repeat.
- **A fact about every random map so far:** before G6 the random seat's X SPELLS cast with whatever X was left on the card — J158's rows show Fireball cast at 0 to 8 — so the random maps' X plays were never a random X. D471 says atlases before/after are two random policies, never pooled, J158's included.
- **G6:** for a play of its own with {X} in its mana cost the random seat draws X uniformly from 1 to Forge's `setMaxXValue`; a play payable only at X = 0 is left out of its options; when the seat picks Forge's own answer Forge's X stays, 0 included. No repeat cap (it would limit legal paid repeats, and nothing loops without it). Counters `xDrawn`, `zeroXLeftOut`; the violation `zeroX` = one of the seat's own X plays that landed on the stack with X < 1, read back from the stack item (the original's X is cleared — the first version read it and counted a false violation); in the seat's log summary (protocol M73), the redaction checker accepts the names, `checks.guardrails` reports them and any `zeroX` > 0 counts under `violationsBy.G6` and fails the atlas. atlas-selftest gets a G6 leg on the Crypt Rats pair; **the G5 leg now plays 16 rollouts** (G6 changed that pair's games — Walking Ballista and Hangarback Walker — and its first refusal came at rollout 16).

### 2026-10-24 04:42 UTC — **mtg-table #194 `pc/atlas-fork-altcost` (D472, amending D466 point 1): the fork rebuilds Forge's alternative-cost plays — J158 pauper5 d12's 240/240 failures**
- **Cause at file:line:** `SearchPolicy.chosenOptionalCosts` (SearchPolicy.java:802 at d82d36e2), reached from `mapSa` (:738–739) via `PriorityCand.install` (:599). Forge built the AltCost copy in `GameActionUtil.getAlternativeCosts` (GameActionUtil.java:118) → `StaticAbilityAlternativeCost.alternativeCosts`, a copy with the cost replaced and marked `OptionalCost.AltCost` (:80); mapSa's description match found the right copy, then D466's re-adding asked `getOptionalCostValues` to offer AltCost — which it never offers — and threw. **A latent second fault:** flashback, harmonize, mayhem, foretold and plotted copies keep the base's description, so mapSa found them only by candidate order.
- **Fix:** mapSa builds the copy's candidates with the same Forge calls that built the original (`getAdditionalCostSpell`, then `getAlternativeCosts`) and requires the SAME KIND of play, not the same description — same alternative cost (AltCost or not), may-play or not, without-mana-cost or not, no optional cost already on it — then adds D466's optional costs back minus AltCost (a kicked flashback maps as both). **Covered:** AltCost statics on the card (115 cards at 2.0.14; four in the lab decks — Snuff Out, Force of Will, Fireblast, Pyrokinesis) and on other cards; flashback, harmonize, mayhem, escape, foretold, plotted; may-play permissions; the card's own alternatives (evoke, dash, overload, prowl, surge, spectacle, bestow…); alternate additional costs; all of D466's optional costs. **Not covered:** madness (cast as its trigger resolves, never at priority). A play the copy cannot rebuild stays a BRANCH ERROR naming its kind, not D470's "unavailable" — the copy is a faithful copy of the same position, so a miss there is a tool fault. **Every search seat goes through mapSa: search-v2 before/after not pooled** (D472 and the ladder guide say so).
- **Tests:** ai-bench selftest 109 checks (new engine case `altcost-map`: Snuff Out's AltCost maps to the copy's "{0}, Pay 4 life" play targeting the copy's Bears, the mana-cost cast to the mana play; Think Twice's flashback → flashback, Mulldrifter's evoke → evoke; an atlas branch forcing the AltCost cast plays clean; a playout leaves the copy at 16 life with the Bears dead and the real game untouched; against the old mapSa the case throws J158's exact exception); bridge/build.sh green; atlas-selftest --jobs 1 OK; **replay of d12 branch 0:** the AltCost Snuff Out plays in both continuations (Forge 23 turns, a win for the seat; random 25; the frame log shows life 15 → 11 and no mana tapped), every other option clean. No full gate, no spine rerun. **Four atlas PRs now wait on you: #192 (G6), #193 (D470 LOWs), #194 (D472)** — J160 pins their merge.

one-worker reproduction (var/j167-s04-diag/): **a Forge 2.0.14 bug** — `ChooseSourceEffect.resolve` loops

the board, Forge answers the divider (or null) ~20,000 times a second, each re-ask a decision record (seat-1 frame
logs of 0.25–1.45 GB, 18 of them) and no game event, so D467's StepClock never reads the 120-s deadline. Upstream
fixed it in 746455d75 (2026-09-15), after 2.0.14; 62 pinned scripts use the same ChooseSource logic. **D484**
(lane running): the random seat answers a ChooseSource as upstream's fix does; every decision callback throws
